# FIZY BOT: notes for Claude Code

## Working with the owner
- Reply in **Malay** (Bahasa Malaysia), casual and short. Code, comments, commit messages and Telegram texts stay in **English**.
- The owner wants only setups that pass a backtest on the broker's own data ("aku tak perlukan trade yang rugi"). **Since 15 Nov 2026 16:04 MYT: test every module on the LAST 3 MONTHS only, not 2 years** (owner: "lepas ni semua modul test 3 bulan je, tak perlu 2 tahun. save memory kau"; see Research).
  - Test first. Add a setup only if it passes. Remove anything that loses.
  - Say plainly when nothing works.
- Realistic expectations: $100 capital, demo first, then a cent account. Weekly 50% returns are not possible; never build martingale or grid EAs (the owner asked for a gold grid on 2 Nov; it was tested and failed, see the findings).

## Safety rules (always)
- Never ask for, type or commit the MT5 password, login or the Telegram token. They live only in GitHub secrets: `MT5_LOGIN`, `MT5_PASSWORD`, `MT5_SERVER`, `TG_TOKEN`, `TG_CHAT_ID`.
- This repository is **public**. Anything committed must be masked: account numbers (5+ digits), IP addresses and $ balances (`mt5/mask_logs.py`, the masking in the workflows).
- The EA trades demo accounts only (`InpDemoOnly = true`). Do not change that without the owner's explicit go-ahead.

## What runs
- **`bbma_scanner.py` + `.github/workflows/scan.yml`:** Linux scanner loop (Deriv data). **Sends nothing to Telegram since 3 Nov** (owner: drop irrelevant messages): BBMA, M5 scalp, RSI2 and the London-breakout paper alerts are off, the 4-hourly "is alive" heartbeat is off (`HEARTBEAT_HOURS = []`) and data-problem messages only go out while a scanner alert is on. It keeps running for `mt5_watchdog()`, which restarts the MT5 shift chain if no shift is running.
- **`.github/workflows/mt5_trade.yml`:** the auto trader. A Windows runner installs the JustMarkets MT5 terminal, compiles `mt5/FizyLB.mq5` and runs it for about 335 minutes. `mt5/next_shift.py` then dispatches the next shift, so it runs 24/7.
  - `mt5_trade.txt`: line 1 `on`/`off`. Lines 2–7 are the lots for London breakout, NR7, Gotobi, gold auction, month-end fix and volatility breakout. Line 8 `on`/`off` switches the gold S/R zone alerts (**off since 2 Nov evening**: no S/R level passed). Line 9 is the lot of the USDJPY day trades (`FizyDTJ.mqh`). Line 10 is the lot of the gold H1 Ichimoku cloud breakout (`FizyKUMO.mqh`). Line 11 is the lot of the gold H1 Tenkan / Kijun cross day trade (`FizyTK.mqh`). Line 12 is the lot of the gold M1 spike fade (`FizySPK.mqh`). Line 13 is the lot of the gold M1 leg fade (`FizyLEG.mqh`). Line 14 is the lot of the gold M1 Heikin Ashi module (`FizyHA.mqh`, v2.08). **All lots are 0.01 since 3 Nov** (owner's request; London was 0.05). Lines 2, 3, 7 and 9 are inert while their modules are off (below).
  - `mt5/watch.py` forwards the EA event files (`MQL5/Files/*_events.txt`, with PNG screenshots) to Telegram, with emoji blocks and MYT times. It also sends one "BOT ONLINE" daily status (`status/mt5_online.txt` holds the date sent). **`STATUS_KINDS` must list exactly the kinds that are actually sent**: the status waits for all of them and otherwise falls back to a 120 s timeout, which truncates it (that is why the 5 Nov morning status was missing blocks — SR and four off modules were still listed).
- **Round 131, ten ideas to raise FLIP's profit, on real ticks** (`research/ticks/ticksim2.c` + `run2.py` + `w1.py` / `w2.py` / `w3.py`, results `research/status/ticks131.txt`, 16 Nov; owner: "kau tak ada idea lain ke. cuba la fikir sehabis mungkin"; build `gcc -O2 -shared -fPIC -o ticksim2.so ticksim2.c -lm`, run locally with `xau.npz`). Base = FROZEN D $4 (round 130): 383 trades PF 1.82, +$948, DD −$91, worst day −$47 / previous 369 trades PF 1.55, +$608, DD −$108. **The one that holds: skip the day when YESTERDAY'S D1 BODY (|close − open|) is bigger than $60** (median body $38, 71% of days are ≤ $60): **226 trades (5 a day on 44 of 63 days), win 12%, PF 2.40, stress 2.13, +$914, DD −$65, worst day −$47 / previous 225 trades PF 2.06, +$698, DD −$55, worst day −$40**; cap $40 / 50 / 60 / 70 / 80 / 100 → 2.50 / 2.38 / 2.40 / 2.09 / 2.10 / 2.03 and 2.40 / 2.24 / 2.06 / 1.83 / 1.75 / 1.68 (a plateau, every cap beats the base); bands on their own $0-20 / 20-40 / 40-60 / 60-90 / 90+ → 3.20 / 1.49 / 1.90 / 1.25 / 0.76 and 2.12 / 2.77 / 1.59 / 0.44 / 1.13; **the big-body days alone: 157 trades (8 a day), PF 1.07, +$34, DD −$197 / 0.80, −$90** (they add trades and drawdown, no money); controls: 6 random masks skipping 30% of days 1.38-1.97 / 1.37-1.70 (the filter beats all six in both windows), the same days WITH the colour 1.08 / 1.00, both sides 1.71 / 1.38, big days with the colour 0.40 / 1.08 (not tradable either way). Only ~19 skipped days a window, so a small sample. With it D $3 / 4 / 5 / 6 → 2.00 / 2.40 / 2.39 / 1.97 and 1.68 / 2.06 / 1.79 / 1.77. **Second, weaker: add 0.01 to a WINNER once at +$10 (own $4 stop; not a martingale)**: alone 1.78 / 1.43, +$1,194 / +$651, DD −$108 / −$187; with the body filter 294 trades PF 2.16 / 2.03, +$1,062 / +$913, DD −$104 / −$102 — more money, drawdown back to about the account; more adds or closer adds (every +$5, max 2-4) lower the previous window to 1.11-1.30 with DD −$280 to −$368. No gain: SL to entry + $0.50 at +$5 / 8 / 10 (previous 1.07-1.28; at +$15-30 ≈ base); time stop 5-120 min (≈ base); max 1-8 entries a day (less money, previous 0.96-1.52); entry cutoff 06:00-20:00 (18:00: 2.00 / 1.51, noise); closing at 16-22:00 (less money); starting at 03:00-10:00 (worse; 10:00 1.24 / 1.16); body at least $5-50 (worse, monotone: 1.72 → 1.21); re-entry only at a new day high / low (1.69-1.73 / 1.12-1.16); a 5-60 min cooldown after a stop (previous 0.95-1.37). P&L by entry number of the day and by entry hour shows no stable pattern between the windows. Offered: frozen D $4 + the $60 body filter; the +$10 add as an option. **Round 131b (`w4.py`; owner: "aku nak trade setiap hari bro"): trade every day, but on a day after a D1 body > $60 take ONE entry only** (`big_mode` 3, `big_maxn` 1): 245 trades, PF 2.34, stress 2.09, +$961, DD −$69, worst day −$47 / previous 242 trades PF 1.89, +$640, DD −$74, worst day −$40 — about the skip version's numbers while trading every day; max 2 / 3 entries 2.17 / 2.02 and 1.77 / 1.66; big-day K $12 2.31 / 1.69; D $3 / 6 / 8 on big days 1.68-1.85 / 1.38-1.57 (worse); both sides on big days 1.50 / 1.73. Offered: frozen D $4 + max 1 entry after a big day. **Owner's decision (16 Nov 23:13 MYT): "aku nak trade banyak kali guna modul flip. aku tak peduli!" — v2.47 (16 trades a day, loses on ticks: PF 0.91 / 0.86) stays live; the frozen versions trade 6 a day and were declined.**

New `FizyMRT.mqh`, magic 260340: against yesterday's D1 colour (doji = none), from 01:30 server one stop order $3 away on the allowed side opens a basket (stays until filled / 23:00); every $3 against the LAST entry adds the next lot, linear 1/2/3/4/5/6 × 0.01 (max 6 = 0.21 lot; x1.5 gave the same money at 0.31 lot); after each add the TP of every position = the price where the whole basket nets +$3 (swap included), the EA also closes the basket at +$3; NO SL, held overnight (`InpMrtClose2330` = false); re-arm after a TP; new baskets obey `DayPaused()` (`InpDayTarget` = 30), an open basket keeps adding; events `mrt_events.txt` (prefix `MART`: ONLINE, ORDER PLACED, BUY / SELL (basket opened), ADD, BASKET CLOSED, STOP OUT, ORDER DELETED). BTC: 1 of 32 (cvd1440 -> 12 h, IC +0.115 / +0.045), but that CVD correlates 0.75-0.80 with the 24 h price move and its part beyond price is +0.093 / -0.036 (does not repeat); 1 of 160 cells is chance level.** No CVD rule to build; the owner was told.

## How to deploy an EA change
1. Edit `mt5/*.mq*`.
2. Touch `run_mt5.txt` and push. `.github/workflows/mt5.yml` compiles everything and writes `status/mt5_check.txt`. Check for `0 errors`.
3. `git rm status/snap_TEST_*.png`, the test screenshots that the check commits.
4. Cancel the running `mt5_trade.yml` run. Its "Next shift" step starts a new shift on the latest `main`. To force a fresh daily status, write any other text into `status/mt5_online.txt` before cancelling.
- **Ghost shift (6 Nov):** run 42066887371 sat `queued` with no jobs from 5 Nov 16:40Z, cannot be cancelled (409), and nothing traded for ~13 h; the watchdog did not restart it. Fix: dispatch `mt5_trade.yml` again, the new run starts. After a deploy, always check that a shift is `in_progress`. **Root cause found 8 Nov: that ghost (still `queued`) made `mt5_watchdog()` see an "active" run forever, so it never restarted the chain** — e.g. after run 42368846500 died on 7 Nov 16:14Z ("The hosted runner lost communication with the server", a GitHub-side failure) nothing restarted it for 2 h. Fixed in `bbma_scanner.py`: a queued run counts as active for 30 min only and a running one for 7 h, and a failed shift is retried after 15 min (was 1 h).
- The GitHub CLI's `gh workflow run` may fail. Use REST instead: `gh api -X POST repos/<owner>/fizy-bot/actions/workflows/<file>/dispatches -f ref=main`, and `.../actions/runs/<id>/cancel`.

## Research (backtests on the broker's own data)
- **`.github/workflows/mt5_data.yml`:** exports M5 history with `mt5/ExportM5.mq5` (input `symbols`, default 30 instruments) into the artifact `m5data`, then runs `research/<script>.py` on Linux. Results go to `research/status/<script>.txt`.
  - Re-run a script on an earlier export with the inputs `script=research/x.py` and `data_run=<run id>`.
  - Input `tf=M1` exports M1 instead of M5 (use with `symbols=XAUUSD`; gold M1 from 2020 is about 2.3 M bars; latest export run **43620182422, 15 Nov, from 2024.09.01** (43406389671 and 43450356142 deleted) (43178601381 deleted; 42730517413, 42511147535 and 42355999555 were deleted) — earlier M1 runs 41663561048, 41872821553 and 42152985627 were deleted).
  - Exports: 12 instruments (XAUUSD, EURUSD, GBPUSD, USDJPY, AUDUSD, USDCAD, USDCHF, NZDUSD, EURJPY, GBPJPY, EURGBP, BTCUSD), 2020-03 → 2026-11-03, in run 41667343723. **The 30-instrument run 41443785106 was deleted (with its artifact) on 3 Nov, like 41277285458 before it: workflow runs are being deleted outside this session, so re-export when an artifact is missing.** WTI, Brent and XNG in run 41330505649. The older 30-instrument export (run 41277285458) was deleted on 2 Nov.
- **Economic calendar:** `mt5/ExportCal.mq5` + `.github/workflows/mt5_cal.yml` → `data/calendar.csv`. Past times are stored at today's server offset (+2 h): treat them as UTC+2, then convert to Europe/Athens.
- **TEST WINDOW (current, owner 15 Nov 2026 16:04 MYT): the LAST 3 MONTHS only** ("lepas ni semua modul test 3 bulan je, tak perlu 2 tahun. save memory kau"). Judge on the last 3 months; show the 3 months before next to it as a repeat check (not a pass condition), and always a random / fake control. Pass rule on the 3 months: PF ≥ 1.15 after spread, stress (1.5× spread) ≥ 1.05, net positive, at least about 30 trades (fewer → say the sample is too small), and report the worst day and the max drawdown against the $100 account. Say plainly when 3 months is too few trades to tell (rounds 22 / 31 / 32: a short window alone does not predict the next one). The 2-year rule below is history.
- **Previous window: the last 1-2 years only (owner, 12 Nov 2026: "tak perlu uji sampai 6 tahun ... nak uji pun cukup dalam 1-2 tahun! ingat pesan aku ni").** Do not run or quote 6.6-year results any more; the older findings below that use 6.6 years are history. Default: the last 2 years, its two years as the halves, plus the last 3 months with the 3 months before as a repeat check. When tuning settings, choose them on the earlier part of that window and judge them on the last 6 months.
- **Previous 2-year pass rule (history, replaced by the 3-month rule above):** ≥ 150 trades, PF ≥ 1.15 after spread, both years (halves) ≥ 1.05, stress (1.5× spread) ≥ 1.05, last 12 months positive.
  - Always compare against a random-entry baseline or fake event times.
  - Drop trades in the 23:45–01:30 server rollover; the bid is distorted there.
- **Findings, short version:**
  - **Edges:**
    - Tokyo-fix flows: Gotobi and month-end.
    - **Gold M1 spike fade, the only M1 edge found (v1.97, rounds 37-39 and 44; `scalpcost37.py`, `spike38.py`, `spike39.py`, `spk44.py`)**: BUY when an M1 bar falls ≥ **2.5** ATR(M1) (3.0 before v1.98) and ATR(M1) ≥ 8 × the spread; SL 0.2 ATR below its low, **no TP**, out after **45 min**. At k 3.0: 1,059 trades, PF 1.53, halves 1.54 / 1.53, stress 1.46, +$1,163 per 0.01 lot, last 12 months +$933, max DD −$144 (recovered in 20 days), 33 of 67 months positive; by year 1.16 / 1.87 / **2.04 (2022, a down year for gold)** / 1.06 / 1.27 / 1.08 / 1.76 — every year above 1, so it is not just the uptrend. Win rate 20%, average win $15.81 against an average loss of $2.58, longest losing run 27 trades. **The TP was removed in round 44**: the sweep is 0.5 / 0.75 / 1.0 × range → 1.30 / 1.39 / 1.33 against 1.5 / 2 / 3 / 5 / none → 1.49 / 1.47 / 1.51 / 1.53 / 1.53, so the old 0.75 sat in the worst zone; the hold plateau is 30-120 min → 1.51-1.64 and 45 was taken over the 90-min peak (PF 1.64) because its halves are level and every year clears 1.06. **k is NOT a plateau** (2.0 / 2.5 / 3.0 / 3.5 / 4.0 → 1.12 / 1.34 / 1.53 / 1.34 / 1.44), so the exact 1.53 was partly luck while the family holds across k 2.5-4.0.
      - **Round 45 (`research/spk45.py`) lowered k to 2.5, live as v1.98** — the owner's call, asked and answered: "asalkan aku nak ea trade banyak kali dan profit". **Settings now: k 2.5, gate 8, SL 0.2 ATR, no TP, 45 min.** 2,230 trades (1.31 a day), PF 1.34, halves 1.47 / 1.33, stress 1.27, +$1,445, last 12 months +$1,049, max DD −$162 (recovered in 2 days), 44 of 75 months positive, every year ≥ 1.09.
      - **METHOD NOTE, reusable: a lower threshold cannot be judged by a sweep, because the lower setting is a SUPERSET of the higher one.** k 2.5's +$1,445 already contained the +$1,163 k 3.0 was making, so every sweep cell looked good for the wrong reason. The decisive test is each band measured ON ITS OWN, non-overlapping, so no band can borrow another's profit: **2.0-2.5 → PF 0.96 (−$193), 2.5-3.0 → 1.17 (+$351), 3.0-3.5 → 1.89 (+$763), 3.5-4.0 → 1.14 (+$61), 4.0-5.0 → 0.89 (−$54)**. The real edge is 3.0-3.5; the band k 2.5 adds does pass on its own, but thinly (PF 1.17 against the 1.15 threshold, stress 1.10 against 1.05) and **on its own it draws down $256 and never recovers on $351 of profit**. It only works blended with the strong core, which fills its gaps: the blend draws down $162 and recovers in 2 days, and profit per $ of drawdown is 8.9 against 8.1 for k 3.0 alone. **The band below 2.5 loses, so 2.5 is a floor, not a step on a ramp** — do not go lower. With the old TP 0.75 / 20 min exit the 2.5-3.0 band was only 1.09, so v1.97 is what made v1.98 possible.
      - Costs of v1.98, all put to the owner first: PF 1.53 → 1.34 and the longest losing run 27 → 36 trades. Plateau at k 2.5: SL 0.1-0.75 ATR → 1.23-1.34, hold 20-120 min → 1.26-1.38, gate 6-16 → 1.21-1.50 and monotone to 12, every cell passing. Controls: random 0.84-1.21, UP side 0.95, the 2.5-3.0 band on the UP side 0.96. The one-position-at-a-time rule discards only 1.5-2.7% of signals, so the 45-minute hold is not what caps the trade count. **Plateaus everywhere**: SL 0.1-0.5 ATR → 1.32-1.38, hold 10-45 min → 1.37-1.39, spread cap makes no difference; the gate x = 8-12 → 1.16-1.44. Fading UP spikes is PF 0.92 and is not traded (it only worked in 2022, the one down year, which is the trend signature). **Trade rate rises with gold's volatility** (`research/spk_rate40.py`): 0.10-0.33 a day in 2020-2024, 1.19 in 2025, **2.22 a day in 2026** (582 trades in the last 12 months). Profit is lumpy and concentrated in the volatile months: 2026 made +$526 of the +$675 total, and Jan-Feb 2026 alone +$336. Expect quiet months near zero. **Not a news strategy** (`research/spk_news41.py`): only 3.6% of signals fall within 5 min of a high-impact USD event and 12.5% within 15 min of any high-impact event; the 1,026 non-news trades still score PF 1.32 (+$571). News spikes are the best ones (PF 2.72 within 5 min) but there are only 38 of them. Busiest hours are the NY session (21:00-22:00 MYT, 257 trades) and 09:00 MYT. Some hours lose (07:00 MYT PF 0.30, 16:00 0.43, 18:00 0.46) but each has only 17-37 trades, so no hour filter was added: that would be fitting noise.
    - **Rounds 42-44, gold M1 designed from measurement rather than from rules** (5 Nov; owner: "aku nak kau cipta sendiri scalping di m1 pada gold. ikut perangai gold"). Guessing rules had failed seven rounds, so round 42 (`m1char42.py`) MEASURED the behaviour of 2.26 M M1 bars first, all in $ per 0.01 lot and split into halves, then round 43 (`m1build43.py`) built only on what was found and round 44 (`spk44.py`) confirmed the one survivor.
      - **The cost gate is real and usable in advance, not hindsight.** corr(ATR/spread now, the next 20 min range) = 0.692; corr(ATR now, ATR in 20 min) = 0.945; range/spread rises monotonically with the gate (11 / 24 / 33 / 45 / 66 / 135), both halves agreeing. This is the structural fact behind the whole M1 scalper.
      - **Variance ratio by session**: Asia 01:30-09:00 **0.82** at every horizon (strongest reversion), London 09:00-15:30 **1.02-1.06** (the only trending session), NY overlap 0.95 → 0.86, and under the live gate 0.96 at 1 min → **0.845 at 60 min** (the reversion builds with the horizon). That last number is what produced the v1.97 TP removal.
      - **LESSON: a variance ratio below 1 is NOT a tradable edge.** The Asia z-score fade built on VR 0.82 scored 0.85-0.91, and the SAME rule run in London as a deliberate falsification control, which was supposed to fail, also failed at 0.92-1.00. Both failing means the session finding was not driving anything: VR < 1 is a small variance effect the spread eats. Without that control the 0.82 would have looked like a reason to build.
      - **Cumulative-drop fade (CUMFADE) fails**: fading a drop of k × ATR over the last 5-30 bars gives PF 0.88-1.09 over 12-22 k trades a day. Its gate sweep is properly monotone (0.69 / 0.83 / 0.88 / 0.91 / 0.95 / 0.99 / 1.04) so the cost effect is real, but it never reaches 1.15. Fading ONE 3-ATR bar is a specific event; fading a general decline is not the same thing.
      - Also failed: London momentum / breakout 0.81-0.93, busy-regime long (ATR14/ATR100 ≥ 1.6) 1.01, NY-open range OCO 0.83-0.96, cumulative-RISE fade 0.87.
      - **Streaks are statistically real and economically dead**: 3-8 minutes in a row gives t up to 5.5 but a mean move of only $0.05-0.16 against a $0.19 spread. Not tested further.
      - **Gold does NOT respect round numbers on M1**: the real $1 / $5 / $10 grid gave −$0.005 and a grid shifted by half a step gave +$0.006, |t| < 1.2 on both, over 420-465 k observations. The shifted control behaves identically, which is the whole answer.
      - Round 42 F/H for reference: the most explosive minute of the day is 15:30 server (NY open, mean 1-minute move $2.07 and a $9.57 range over the next 30 min); gold's ATR peaks at 16-17h server ($1.21) and its spread is cheapest then ($0.18).
    - Gold before the LBMA AM auction, **with an M1 trailing exit (v1.94, rounds 34-36)**: sell 10:15 London, SL 2 ATR(M5), from 10:30 trail the stop at the lowest M1 low + 1.0 ATR(M1), hard exit 10:45. 1,703 trades, PF 1.66, halves 1.26 / 1.91, stress 1.47, +$762 per 0.01 lot, last 12 months +$542, max DD −$83, 52 of 81 months positive; by year 2.32 / 1.41 / 0.91 / 0.84 / 1.06 / 1.73 / 2.75. The old 10:30 exit was PF 1.48 with a failing first half (0.98). Plateau check: every fixed exit 10:31-10:37 passes (PF 1.45-1.66) and every trail 0.3-2.0 ATR passes (1.49-1.68), so it is not one lucky setting.

Live since v1.90. **Re-check with the broker's real swap (round 23, −71 / −84 points a night per lot, about −7% a year both ways): PF 1.21, halves 1.00 / 1.32, +$1,330 incl. −$542 swap: first half fails.** Kept for now (net positive); the owner was told.

Buys 1.55, sells 1.43; neighbours 7/22/44 1.33, 12/30/60 1.27, no cloud filter 1.22, SL 1.5 / 3 ATR 1.59 / 1.45; daily P&L correlation with Kumo 0.11. Live since v1.92.

Trading the previous 2 weeks' best rule, out of sample: -3363 $ over 172 periods (31% positive), every year negative.
    - Round 33, an M1 method designed from the data rather than copied (`research/m1design33.py`, 4 Nov; owner: "dapatkan cara paling sesuai ... berdasarkan pemerhatian kau"): a scan of EVERY entry minute of the day x holds of 5/10/15/30/60 min, long and short, on the London and the New York clock (11,930 windows), two-stage (first half |t| ≥ 3.5, then second half t ≥ 2 and PF ≥ 1.1): **0 survivors, while a day-shuffled control gave 22** — there is no second time window like the LBMA auction, and even the live auction edge would not pass this filter (its first half is only 1.04). Big-spike fade with a wide stop, 3-6 ATR: best PF 0.97. High-impact USD news fade / follow from `data/calendar.csv`: both 0.98. What did work was M1 timing on the auction scalp, detailed in rounds 34-36 (now live).
    - Round 35's trailing exit first scored PF 2.09 with a $33 drawdown; that was intrabar look-ahead (the same bar's low set the trail before its high was checked). Fixed in `gau_trail36.py`: the trail level is frozen at the start of each bar from the previous bars' lows. **Always set a trailing level from closed bars only.**
    - Round 37, **the cost gate** (`research/scalpcost37.py`, 5 Nov; owner: "aku nak scalper"): the one angle never tried on M1 was filtering by cost. The export carries every bar's spread, so each rule was re-run under filters (cheapest 25% / 10% of spreads, ATR(M1) ≥ 5 / 8 / 12 × that bar's spread, London+NY hours) and at fixed cost levels, reporting the break-even cost for PF 1.15. A plain spread filter does nothing, but **ATR ≥ 8 × spread turns the M1 spike fade from PF 0.80 into 1.16** while random entries under the same gate score 0.71. The filter sweep is monotone (x = 0, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 12 → PF 0.80, 1.02, 1.05, 1.07, 1.11, 1.16, 1.18, 1.26, 1.44), which is the signature of a real cost effect.
    - **Round 46, "an EA that trades EVERY MINUTE" (`research/everymin46.py`, 5 Nov; owner: "sekarang aku nak ea yang trade setiap minit"). NOT POSSIBLE, and this is the definitive answer — do not re-test it, point here.**
      - **The cost wall:** gold M1 has **1,370 bars a trading day** and a $0.19 median spread, so entering every minute bills **$66,376 a year** in spread alone at 0.01 lot. Every edge this project has ever found, added together, is about **$900 a year**. An every-minute EA needs **74×** the total edge of everything that works, just to break even.
      - **Traded literally** (every bar, 1-minute hold, 2.33 M trades): always long, always short, random, follow the last minute, fade the last minute, follow the EMA20 slope — all **PF 0.44-0.45, about −$76,000 a year**. "Fade the last minute" does have a real gross edge of +$6,418, but that is **$0.0028 a trade against a $0.19 spread**: 1.5% of the cost. Staying in the market and paying only on signal flips still flips 194-686 times a day and still loses $82 k-$262 k.
      - **THE DECISIVE PROOF — the predictability ceiling.** A 20-feature linear model of the next minute's return (past returns, ATR, spread, gate, range, body, momentum, EMA distance, hour) fitted **IN SAMPLE on all 2.33 M bars**, i.e. deliberately cheating: **in-sample R² = 0.000365**, mean |predicted move| **$0.0102 against a $0.19 spread**, prediction exceeds the spread on **0.1%** of bars, and trading its sign costs −$498,419 (PF 0.456). A **saturated** bucket model (sign of last move × gate tercile × hour, 132 buckets, can fit any shape, a higher ceiling still): mean |bucket mean| **$0.0062**, and a bucket mean exceeds the spread on **367 of 2.33 M bars (0.02%)**. Scored honestly (buckets learned on the first half, applied to the second): **PF 1.000 and −$0.0000 a trade BEFORE costs** — so there is no gross edge at the 1-minute horizon at all, not merely one the spread eats. Lag-1 autocorrelation of 1-minute returns is −0.0059.
      - **THE FRONTIER, the useful positive answer.** Sweeping the spike fade's k from 5.0 to 0.5 ATR and the gate from 12 to 0: PF falls monotonically as the trade rate rises, and **the highest trade rate that still passes the pass rule is 1.31 a day — exactly the live v1.98 setting (k 2.5, gate 8)**. The near misses are worse on BOTH counts: k 2.0/gate 8 gives 3.17 a day at PF 1.12 and +$1,138, and k 2.5/gate 4 gives 3.95 a day at PF 1.13 and +$1,080, both below the live +$1,445. So v1.98 sits at the top of the frontier and there is nothing to trade up to.
    - **Round 47, the seven M1 angles never tried (`research/wild47.py`, 5 Nov; owner: "ciptakan aku lagi ea scalping yang paling gila di m1"). 0 new rules pass, but four findings worth keeping.**
      - **Spread shock fails, and the reason matters: the shock IS the cost.** After a bar whose spread is 5-10x its own daily median, a down bar reverses +$3.40 over 45 min and an up bar continues +$3.90 — both point UP, so it is gold's drift, not a liquidity effect. And the spread at that moment is **$1.39-$2.00** instead of $0.19, so trading it gives PF 0.11-0.60. **The cost gate already filters these out**: 2,181 of the live rule's 2,230 trades fire on a bar with spread < 1.2x its median, and the 1.2-2x and >= 2x buckets are too small to test. The existing design was already doing this.
      - **The buy-side fade is NOT a bull-market artefact.** Split by the 20-day daily trend it scores 1.30 when the trend is DOWN and 1.37 when it is UP — it works in both. Good robustness check on the live rule.
      - **Round 42's claim about UP spikes is confirmed and still not tradable.** Fading up spikes: all days 0.95, 20-day trend DOWN **1.04**, trend UP 0.87. So the continuation really is a trend effect, exactly as claimed — and 1.04 still fails the 1.15 threshold. The explanation was right and produces no edge.
      - **High tick volume makes the live rule WORSE, not better**: LIVE with volume < 2x its 60-bar mean scores 1.39, at 2-4x only 1.23, and standalone volume spikes fail (vol >= 3x with gate 8: halves 0.56 / 1.73, first half fails). Round 16's M5 finding holds on M1 even with the gate.
      - Clusters: all subsets pass but none beats taking them all (within 10 min 1.64 on n=332 with 2021 at 6.40 and 2023 at 0.00 = noise; isolated > 2 h 1.39 on n=1,269). No filter added.
      - Minute-of-N microstructure: the first minute of a 5 / 15 / 60-minute block scores 1.47 / 1.60 / 1.89 against 1.36 / 1.32 / 1.32 for the middle, a consistent gradient — but mean |1-minute move| is also highest there ($0.637 at position 0 of the hour against $0.494 at position 13), so it is most likely the gate effect measured a different way. Using it as a filter would CUT trades, which is the opposite of what the owner wants, and the by-year numbers are unstable (0.64 / 1.78 / 2.83 / 0.65 / ...). Not added.
      - Regime: the live rule is best at ATR14/ATR100 of 1.2-2.0 (PF 1.52, 45 of 72 months positive) and fails above 2.0 (0.85, n=71). By gate band: 8-10 → 1.14, 10-14 → 1.35, 14-20 → 1.64, >= 20 → 1.39. Raising the gate to 10 would lift PF to about 1.44 but cut the trade rate from 1.31 to 0.82 a day, so it was not done.
      - **The one near miss, NOT added: re-entry after a stop.** Re-entering once at the next bar's open after the live rule is stopped gives, combined, 3,812 trades (2.24 a day), PF 1.20, halves 1.16 / 1.21, stress 1.14, +$1,561, last 12 months **+$1,435** — which passes. But by the round-45 method the added component must be measured ALONE, and **re-entries on their own are PF 1.09, which fails**, while profit per $ of drawdown falls from 8.9 to 5.8 (DD −$162 → −$271) and three years drop below 1. So it is exposure, not edge. Offered to the owner with that recommendation against; he can still override as he did for the M15 TK cross.
    - **Rounds 48-49, the owner's complaint that 1.31 trades a day is not a scalper** (`research/scalp48.py`, `gate49.py`, 5 Nov: "sepanjang market ... naik turun hampir 100 pip, apa ea sekarang buat? perati je? ... 1 hari aku tunggu trade langsung tak ada, kalau ada pun 1 2 je"). He is right about the symptom; the answers are all measured.
      - **RANGE SCALPING IS DEAD on M1.** Buying the bottom and selling the top of the day's own realised range so far (bands of 10 / 20 / 30%, gated, SL 1 ATR, TP 1 ATR, 30 min, 7-98 trades a day): **PF 0.77-0.95 at a ~50% win rate**, every variant losing, and most of them **worse than the random control (0.89)**. Also dead against a rolling 60 / 240-bar Donchian position (0.86-0.95) and when restricted to days that have already moved more than 4 ATR, which was literally his case. A 50% win rate means the direction is a coin flip and the spread is the whole result.
      - **LOWERING THE GATE BELOW 8 ADDS LOSING TRADES.** Same superset trap as k, same band test (`gate49.py`). Non-overlapping gate bands at k 2.5: **4-6 → PF 0.90 (−$37/yr), 6-8 → PF 0.90 (−$29/yr, drawdown −$291 NEVER recovered, last 12 months −$232)**, 8-10 → 1.14 (+$25), 10-14 → 1.35 (+$69), 14-20 → 1.64 (+$80), ≥20 → 1.39 (+$53). Cumulative gate ≥ 6 still "passes" at PF 1.21 only because the strong high-gate bands carry the losing one. **Gate 8 is a floor. Do not lower it.**
      - **The near-target version, which is what "untung makan sikit-sikit" means**: the same entries with TP 0.5 × the spike range trade the same number of times but win 36% instead of 17% with a smaller drawdown (−$101 vs −$162) for less than half the profit ($82/yr vs $216/yr). At k 2.5 gate 8 that version passes (PF 1.18). At k 2.0 it is 3.37 a day, win 37.4%, 48 of 80 months positive, PF 1.08 — **the owner chose that one, live as v1.99**.
    - **Round 50, TKM (Teknik Keabadian Momentum, myHOBI) on M1 with the cost gate** (`research/tkm50.py`, 6 Nov; owner: "kau tengok balik teknik tkm yang aku pernah share dulu. cuba faham lepas tu modified sampai jadi"). **0 of ~60 variants pass. The levels carry no information at all.** Two things were open in the earlier M15 test and both are now closed: it had never been run on M1, and it predated the cost gate.
      - **THE CONTROL IS THE ANSWER.** The same rule with the level anchor **moved +3 ATR** scores PF 0.90 and **moved +7 ATR** scores 0.91, against **0.91** for the real EMA 63 levels; random entries under the same gate score 0.87. Moving the levels anywhere changes nothing, so a TKM level is just "somewhere away from a moving average" and the distance is arbitrary. Same control that killed S/R zones and micro round numbers.
      - **The win-rate trap, worth understanding.** As taught (no stop, TP 125-320 points back toward the MA) it wins **86-90% of trades** and still scores PF 0.69-0.88, losing $116-$318 a year per 0.01 lot. Winning almost every trade and losing everything on the rare one is the signature of a no-stop counter-trend scalp. That is why it feels right and why it empties an account.
      - Every modification was tried and none helped: levels as a share of price (0.91-0.92) or in ATR (0.94-0.95, and 1.09 on only 173 trades with halves 0.92/1.13); the CCI window kept / loosened / removed (0.91-0.96); a real ATR stop (0.91-0.96); holds 24 / 60 / 120 min (0.91); with-trend instead of counter (0.72-0.83, worse). The gate sweep IS monotone (0.76 / 0.80 / 0.85 / 0.87 / 0.91 / 0.96 / 0.97 for gate 0 / 2 / 4 / 6 / 8 / 12 / 16) so the cost effect is real — it just tops out below 1.0.
      - The one TKM piece that was positive on M15, the EMA 63/83 cross, on M1 gated: best is PF 1.19 at gate 12 / SL 3 ATR but the **first half is 0.88** and three years are 0.15-0.62. At gate 8 it is 1.08 with the first half at 1.04. Fails.
    - **Round 51, TKM thought through properly** (`research/tkm51.py`, 6 Nov; owner: "kau fikir lebih dalam bagaimana nak modified supaya profitable"). Round 50 only killed TKM's levels as a trigger; this measured its other two components and tried all three as FILTERS on the rule that works. **Nothing survives, but it produced the structural reason why every counter-trend level technique tested in this project has lost.**
      - **TKM'S CORE CLAIM IS BACKWARDS IN THE ZONE THAT IS TRADABLE.** Measured forward move from a given distance from the EMA 63, in ATR, gate >= 8, signed so + means a move back toward the MA: **−3..−1.5 ATR → −$0.223 at 30 min (t −3.7, halves −0.209 / −0.226), −5..−3 → −$0.214 (t −2.8, halves −0.250 / −0.206), +1.5..+3 → −$0.197 (t −3.8, halves −0.072 / −0.214)**. Negative means price keeps going AWAY from the MA. Consistent in both halves, three independent buckets. **On gold M1, being stretched predicts MORE stretch, not reversion** — which is why TKM, the S/R flip (round 20), S/R zones, round numbers and range scalping (round 48) have all lost. It is one fact, not five coincidences.
      - The reversion TKM relies on does exist, but only at extreme distance and only in the second half: below −8 ATR gives +$4.07 at 30 min with halves **+0.686 / +5.666**, and 3..5 ATR gives +$0.277 with halves **−0.343 / +0.389**. That is the bull market, not an edge. Note also the magnitudes in the tradable buckets are $0.20-0.22 against a $0.19 spread, so even trading the continuation direction leaves about $0.02 a trade.
      - **TKM's bounded CCI band 175-233 is NOT special, and this is now definitive.** Forward move at 30 min, gate >= 8, signed so + means the extreme reverted: |CCI| 100-150 → −$0.051, 150-175 → −$0.002, **175-233 (the taught band) → −$0.057**, 233-300 → −$0.502, >300 → −$0.413. The taught band sits exactly on the interpolation of its neighbours: no discontinuity, nothing that makes 175-233 different from 150-175 or 233-300, and every value far below the $0.19 spread. The one genuinely odd thing in TKM is not odd.
      - As filters on the live spike fade, nothing holds up. The cells that "pass" fail on inspection: `dist −5..−2` reaches PF 1.15 but cuts 66% of the trades for a lower net; `|CCI| 175-233` is 1.16 on 464 trades with 2023 at 0.22 and 2024 at 0.35; `CCI <= −175` is 1.21 on 530 trades with the same two dead years. `SKIP |CCI| > 300` looked best because it costs only 2% of trades, but it improves the k 2.5 variant by $4 over 6.7 years while making the live k 2.0 variant WORSE (1.08 → 1.07, +$549 → +$444) — it fits one variant and hurts the other, so it fails the consistency test it was given. No filter added.
    - **Rounds 52-53, spike fade with a rejection-wick confirmation** (`research/spkconf52.py`, `spkconf53.py`, 6 Nov). Triggered by live v1.99 buying four times in six minutes on 5 Nov (16:38-16:44 server, gold 4124 → 4099, every trade stopped, −$14) and the owner sending the classic spike-fade method. The method as written (wait W bars for a lower wick ≥ 0.5-0.6 of the range, SL beyond the wick, TP at the pre-spike level) **fails alone** (PF 0.92-1.08), the half-at-1:1 + breakeven exit does not rescue it, the **sell side fails everywhere** (0.85-0.89) and a 30-min cooldown alone does not help (0.91). **Adding RSI(14) < 30 at the spike makes it pass**, and it survives the checks: RSI < 20 / 25 / 30 → 1.33 / 1.30 / 1.28 (35 / 40 fail), 11 of 12 wait × wick cells pass, k 2.0 / 2.5 / 3.0 → 1.28 / 1.37 / 1.39. Attribution: RSI with no wick wait 1.07, the wick with no RSI 0.95 — **both conditions are needed jointly**; random entries on RSI<30 bars 0.80. Every line also reports the worst single day: **v1.99 lost up to $77.70 in one day, the new rule $45**. Live as v2.00.
      - **LESSON: always report the worst single day and the drawdown against the ACCOUNT SIZE ($100), not just PF.** v1.99 was offered with a −$200 drawdown that is twice the account, and nobody said so until it hurt.
    - **Round 54, how long gold's M1 trends last** (`research/legs54.py`, 6 Nov; owner: "kalau trend sell berapa bilangan candle, kalau trend buy berapa bilangan candle"). Measurement, with a control that shuffles 1-minute returns inside each day. The M1 export run 41872821553 had been deleted, so it was re-exported (run 42152985627, gold M1 2020-03-30 .. 2026-11-06).
      - **Candle-colour runs are a coin flip**: average run 1.95 green / 1.94 red against 1.97 / 1.96 shuffled, every run length within 0.2 points of 0.5^L.
      - **Buy and sell legs are mirror images**: at a 0.10% (~$4) zigzag, both have a median of 14 candles and $5.3, 50.0% of legs are buy legs, and every MYT hour is 49-52% buy. There is no buy/sell asymmetry and no time-of-day direction bias.
      - Real legs are SHORTER and FASTER than shuffled ones (14 vs 17-18 candles, $0.38-0.39 vs $0.30 per candle): moves come in bursts.
      - Hazard (0.10% legs, reaching 10 more candles): fresh legs end a little more often than chance (at 5 candles 58-59% vs 63-64%), mature legs continue a little more (at 45-60 candles 75-79% vs 70-72%). Hindsight-dependent (the leg start is only confirmed after a 0.10% move) and round 51 found the continuation worth about $0.02 a trade after spread, so not built.
      - When it moves: 21:00-00:00 MYT (NY open) has 4.1-4.7 legs an hour with a median of 8-10 candles; 07:00 MYT one slow leg (68 candles); 12:00-13:00 MYT 26-28 candles. 2026 is a different regime: 89 legs a day of 9 candles and $9.6, against 30-55 a day of 14-23 candles and $3.4-4.6 in 2020-2024.
    - **Round 55, round 54's leg pattern traded ONLINE** (`research/legs55.py`, 6 Nov; owner: "kau cipta ea di m1 berkaitan dengan pola dan kekerapan gold ni"). **0 pass; the leg pattern is not tradable.** MATURE (follow a leg once it is N candles old, stop at the zigzag reversal level), zigzag 0.05 / 0.10 / 0.20%, N 10-90, all day / gate ≥ 8 / NY: **PF 0.67-0.99, and every cell is WORSE than the same rule on the day-shuffled path** (e.g. 0.10% N30: real 0.81, shuffled 0.98). The hindsight "mature legs continue" effect does not survive being traded live: the live stop sits a full zigzag step from the extreme and bursts reverse into it. FRESH (fade a newly confirmed leg, TP at its start): 0.77-0.94, **identical to the shuffled control** (0.77 / 0.86 / 0.91), so the "fresh legs die more" effect is worth nothing either; worst days −$93 to −$303 per 0.01 lot, above the $100 account. Do not re-test leg counting or zigzag age.
    - **Round 56, the owner's leg fade with adds and no SL** (`research/legfade56.py`, 6 Nov): see the `FizyLEG.mqh` row. A grid by another name: 84% wins, and every few days one basket wipes the account. Live anyway on his order.
    - **Round 57, Heikin Ashi M1 run counts** (`research/ha57.py`, 6 Nov; owner: "kalau guna heikin ashi?"): HA same-colour runs median 3 candles, p90 8-9, p99 16, max 42 (339 runs a day). Real runs end slightly MORE often than shuffled (22% vs 26-28% go 5 more) but that is worth nothing: a plain fade or follow at run 8-20 (SL/TP 1 ATR, 60 min) scores PF 0.55-0.73, both halves below 1. The owner's basket on HA runs: PF 0.81-1.05, 80-218 stop-outs of the $100 account. No pass; FizyLEG not changed.
    - **Round 58, the owner's 18-24 candle cycle** (`research/cycle58.py`, 6 Nov; owner: every leg lasts 18-24 candles then turns, V / W / M, on every TF — a TKM-style claim, with yellow/aqua line and CCI 130-145 points he asked to ignore). **There is no 18-24 cycle on gold M1.** Leg lengths at 0.10%: 11.0% of legs are 18-24 candles against 13.0% on the shuffled path (FEWER, not more); the histogram falls smoothly from 1-3 candles with no bump anywhere; a leg that reached 18 ends by 24 in 25.9% vs 25.7% shuffled. Without any zigzag, corr(last L candles, next L) is a tiny −0.003 to −0.010 at EVERY L from 6 to 60, with no dip at 18-24. Fading an 18 / 21 / 24-candle move: PF 0.79-0.93 at 8-109 trades a day, **identical to the shuffled control (0.80-0.82)**. Do not re-test fixed candle-count cycles.
    - **Round 62, pullbacks inside one trend** (`research/retrace62.py`, 7 Nov; owner: "dalam 1 trend ada berapa kali dia retrace"): inside a 0.10% wick leg, pullbacks of ≥ $1 / $2 / $3 average 1.54 / 0.63 / 0.31 (shuffled control 1.51 / 0.59 / 0.29, the same); 57% of legs have no $2 pullback. Longer legs hold more $1 pullbacks (1.0 at 1-9 candles → 3.6 at 60+) but not more $2+ ones. 2026: 1.80 / 1.37 / 0.86 per leg, and legs of 20+ candles carry 5.3 / 3.3 / 1.6. A measurement only; matches a random walk.
    - **Round 63, re-entry after the pullback** (`research/reentry63.py`, 7 Nov; owner: "macam mana aku nak reentry selepas retrace"). BREAK (stop order at the leg extreme after its first $1 / $2 / $3 pullback, with the trend, SL beyond the pullback, TP 1R / 2R, 30 min): **PF 0.84-0.94, last 12 months −$946 to −$3,213, 0 pass, and the shuffled path does BETTER (0.96-1.10)**; random direction 0.79-0.88. DIP (limit at the pullback, SL where the leg would end, TP back at / beyond the extreme): PF 0.64-0.76, −$9,700 to −$16,127 a year, worst days −$392 to −$525, same as shuffled. Trend continuation after a pullback is not tradable on gold M1.
    - **Round 64, leg age at the pullback** (`research/ageplay64.py`, 7 Nov; owner: "baca la bilangan candle dah ke berapa, kalau dah hujung² memang la patah balik ... cukup 1 bulan dah" — **he now wants tests judged on ONE MONTH; show the month before next to it as a repeat check**). At the first $1 / $2 pullback, continue (stop at the extreme) if the leg is younger than N = 5 / 10 / 15 / 20 candles, fade it if older. **Every cell loses in BOTH months**: continue-if-young PF 0.81-0.95 (last month −$27 to −$237), fade-if-old PF 0.32-0.77 (−$23 to −$535) — the "old leg is about to turn" idea is the worse half; combined 0.69-0.90, no better than random direction (0.70-0.84).
    - **Round 65, market-structure scalps chosen on the last month** (`research/struct65.py`, 7 Nov; owner: "create ea scalping dalam tempoh 1 bulan dari corak structure gold"). Fractal swings n 3 / 5 / 10 on M1 and M5; BOS, BOS with HH+HL trend, CHoCH, HL / LH entry, SFP sweep; TP 1R / 2R, 60 min, ± gate 8: 102 variants. **17 made money last month (about what chance gives; the random-direction control was above 1 on many of them too), only 3 also the month before, and all 3 lose over 12 months**: CHoCH n10 TP1R gate8 +$27 / +$15 (0.5 a day) but 12 m −$16; CHoCH n3 TP2R +$26 / +$21 (15 a day) but 12 m −$1,007; CHoCH n3 TP1R gate8 +$22 / +$17 (3.5 a day) but 12 m −$629. Last month's best (M1 HL n5 TP2R gate8, +$77) lost $205 the month before. Not built. (The $3 rows have no first-half trades: in 2020-2022 a $3 pullback was bigger than the 0.10% leg reversal.)
    - **Round 66, the owner's Heikin Ashi + EMA 10/30 + Stochastic 14,3,3 rule on M1** (`research/ha66.py`, 9 Nov; judged on 2 months): EMA10 above/below EMA30, an HA indecision candle (shadows both sides, body <= 0.3 / 0.5 of range), then an HA candle with no lower (upper) shadow, %K < 80 (> 20), SL at the 10-bar swing low/high, TP 1 / 1.5 / 2 ATR. **12 of 12 variants lose in BOTH months**: PF 0.75-0.86, -$219 to -$454 a month per 0.01 lot, 25-40 trades a day, worst day -$39 to -$102; random direction 0.79-0.95. **Built on the owner's order (9 Nov, "kau bina ea ni. aku nak test") as a STANDALONE EA `mt5/FizyHA.mq5`** (magic 260322, demo only, not part of `FizyLB.mq5` or the live shift; compiled in `mt5.yml`): defaults are the least-losing cell (body 0.3, TP 1.5 ATR, slope on), every setting an input, for the owner to run in the Strategy Tester or on a demo chart himself.
    - **Round 79 / 79b, a second search for a frequent gold M1 scalper on the 3-month frame** (`research/scalp79.py`, `scalp79b.py`, 10 Nov; owner: "cuba carikan aku scalping gold m1 yang bagus dan trade banyak kali. test dalam 3 bulan ... aku kata cari"). 149 variants of families not run on M1 before (2 / 3-bar drop fade, new exits for the live spike fade, up-spike follow, RSI dip in an H1 trend, pin bar, Keltner re-entry, session-mean band, quiet-then-burst, 3-bar reversal, spike + RSI), with a random-time control (same entries at random bars). 8 pass the 3-month frame; 7 of them are the live spike fade with another exit, the 8th is the Keltner re-entry. Checked on 6.6 years (79b): **(1) spike fade + stop to breakeven after +1 ATR passes the FULL pass rule and beats live on risk**: PF 1.21 (live 1.12), halves 1.06 / 1.22, stress 1.11, +$1,198 (live +$947), max DD −$303 (−$459), worst day −$115 (−$142), last 12 months +$892 (+$629), last 3 months +$127 (+$192), previous 3 +$56 (+$140); breakeven at 0.5 / 1 / 2 / 3 ATR → 1.17 / 1.21 / 1.18 / 1.14, a plateau; win rate falls to 9%. Hold 20 / 90 min instead: 1.07 / 1.10, no gain. **(2) Keltner re-entry BUY** (close back above EMA 20 − 3 ATR after a close below it, gate 8, SL 0.2 ATR under the bar, no TP, 45 min), counted only where the spike fade has no signal within 5 bars: 1,498 trades, PF 1.32, stress 1.26, +$1,030, max DD −$193, worst day −$45, last 3 months +$146, previous 3 +$77, last 12 months +$716 — **but first half 0.94 and 2021 at 0.63: it is a 2025-26 regime effect and fails the pass rule**; band depth is monotone (2 / 2.5 / 3 / 3.5 / 4 ATR → 0.90 / 0.94 / 1.05 / 1.19 / 1.27, first half < 1 at every depth). (3) 2-bar drop ≥ 2.5 ATR: PF 1.28 but it is mostly the spike fade again — on its own signals PF 1.16, +$100, first half 0.93. Spike + breakeven 2 ATR with the Keltner module as a second position: last 3 months +$368, previous 3 +$202, last 12 months +$1,717, max DD −$232, worst day −$103, first half 0.99. Everything else lost or matched the random control. Offered to the owner: breakeven +1 ATR as an upgrade, the Keltner module as an optional second scalper; nothing deployed yet. **Round 79c, Keltner with a breakeven stop** (`research/scalp79c.py`; owner: "kalau keltner buat ada breakeven at least 30 pip?"): SL to entry after +$1 / 2 / 3 / 4 / 5 → PF 1.59 / 1.50 / 1.50 / 1.46 / 1.43 (none: 1.32), a plateau; **+$3 (30 pips): +$1,049, stress 1.40, max DD −$112 (−$193), worst day −$21 (−$45), last 3 months +$156, previous 3 +$108, last 12 months +$781, win 19%** — better on every measure, but the first half stays 0.91 (2020 / 2021 / 2024 below 1), so still a 2025-26 regime rule. Lock +$1 after +$3: about the same (PF 1.48, win 47%). The spike fade with breakeven at +$3: PF 1.17, +$1,006, max DD −$280 (at +1 ATR: 1.21, +$1,198).
    - **Round 83, the owner's BBMA multi-timeframe entry** (`research/bbma83.py`, 12 Nov; owner: "cari mom m15, m5 reject ema50, m1 cari csak dan untuk kuat kan lagi signal csak, kau cari melalui indikator apa yang sesuai"). Definitions from `bbma_scanner.py`: M15 momentum (close beyond the BB, valid 8 M15 bars, cancelled by an opposite one) → M5 EMA 50 rejection after it (touch and close back, previous close on the right side, valid 6 M5 bars) → M1 CSAK (one candle opens on one side of min / max(mid BB, EMA 50) and closes beyond both, not beyond the outer BB); SL beyond the M5 rejection extreme, TP 1R / 2R / M15 outer BB, 120-minute cap, one trade per rejection. **Fails: 1,800 trades (about 1 a day), PF 0.81-0.86, halves 0.72-0.76 / 0.86-0.92, last 12 months −$3 / −$78 / −$111, worse than the same entries with a RANDOM direction (0.90).** CSAK alone on M1 0.80 (18 a day, 12 months −$1,081). Twelve confirmation filters on the CSAK, alone and in pairs (RSI 50, MACD histogram, Stochastic, ADX / DI, CCI 0 / 100, PSAR, Heikin Ashi, body ≥ 1 ATR, cost gate, MA5/10 close): 0.80-0.96 with ≥ 190 trades; the only cell above 1 is CCI > 100 (88 trades in 6.6 years, PF 1.12, first half 0.95, 12 months +$17) — too few and below the bar. Tick volume is not in the export. Not built. **Round 83b / 83c** (`research/bbma83b.py`, `bbma83c.py`; owner: "tak kan sehari 1 trade? ... momentum m15 tu"): the funnel per trading day is 11.7 M15 momentum candles, 24.5 M5 EMA 50 rejections, 39 M1 CSAKs, but only 1.2 rejections a day come after a momentum AND get a CSAK within the windows (8 M15 bars / 6 M5 bars). Longer windows raise the rate and do not help: 4 h / 60 min → 2.2-2.3 a day, PF 0.82-0.83, 12 months −$165 to −$303; 8 h / 2 h → 3.5-3.7 a day, PF 0.84-0.85, −$111 to −$280; 24 h / 4 h → 4.0-4.2 a day, PF 0.83-0.86, −$24 to −$113, first half 0.66-0.69; random direction 0.87-0.90 at every window.
    - **Round 84, the owner's BBMA SOP on its original timeframes + CCI** (`research/bbma84.py`, 12 Nov; owner: "arah h4, ema50 90 jadi penentu. h4 cari candle mom 8 candle lookback. lepas mom, di h1/m30 tunggu reject ema50 ... m15 candle buat csak. gabungkan sekali dengan cci"). H4 EMA 50 vs 90 direction, H4 momentum in the last 8 H4 candles (no opposite momentum, no H4 close back through the H4 EMA 50 since), then an H1 or M30 EMA 50 rejection after it (valid 4 / 8 candles), then an M15 CSAK; CCI(20) on the CSAK > 100 / > 0 / none; SL under the rejection or the CSAK, TP 1R / 2R / H4 BB, 48 h cap, swap. **The full chain is rare: 44-63 trades in 6.6 years with the H1 rejection (0.1-0.2 a week), 116-185 with M30 (0.3-0.5 a week); CCI > 100 almost never coincides with a CSAK (1-5 trades), because a CSAK crosses the mid BB where CCI sits near 0-100.** Results are noise-sized: best H1 rej 8 / SL rejection / TP 2R 57 trades PF 1.20 (halves 0.76 / 1.48, 12 months +$160, 6.6 y +$77); M30 everything 0.49-0.82 except CCI > 0 + SL CSAK + TP 1R (120-140 trades, PF 1.03-1.10, first half 0.80-0.86, 6.6 y +$11 / +$26); random direction on the same entries 0.73-1.15. The M15 CSAK alone (no H4 / rejection context) 2,610 trades PF 0.97. Nothing passes; not built.

Rules from the SMC guides (TradingView mechanical-entry cheatsheet, MQL5 SMC guide, LuxAlgo, XS.com): online fractal swings n 3 / 5; a close beyond the last swing = BOS (with the trend) or CHoCH (against); protected low / high = the extreme of the impulse; sweep = it took the swing before it; OB = last opposite candle at / up to 5 bars before the protected extreme; FVG inside the impulse; limit at OB top / OB 50% / FVG top / FVG 50% / OTE 0.705; cancelled by a close beyond the stop, an opposite break or 30 bars; SL protected extreme + 0.1 ATR + spread; TP 2R / 3R / the impulse high (liquidity); filters HTF structure bias, discount / premium of the HTF dealing range, killzone (fill 09-12 or 14-17 server); setup / bias M5 / H1, M15 / H4, H1 / D1; numba in the runner (`mt5_data.yml` pip line). Every trade has a random-direction twin (and in 102b / c an always-BUY twin) at the same fill bar. **28 forex + gold pooled: 3 of 3,945 variants pass (chance)**, median PF 0.70-0.86 by timeframe vs random 0.64-0.83, every filter adds +0.00-0.03; the textbook models all lose (e.g. BOS + HTF + OB top TP liquidity: M5 0.71, M15 0.74, H1 0.80-0.85; sweep + CHoCH + FVG 50% TP 2R: 0.67 / 0.77 / 0.79); year-1 picks → year 2 median 0.83. Forex fails on cost: risk 0.6-3 $ per 0.01 lot against 1-3 pips of spread. **Gold alone (102b): 57 of 1,369 pass**, always-buy twin below the signal (median 0.93), sells 0.99 vs buys 0.91 (median). **The gold family that holds (102c): M15 setup, BOS only, killzone LDN+NY, limit at the OB top, TP 3R, swing n 5** — 189 trades (1.8 a week), PF 1.67, years 1.64 / 1.72, stress 1.64, +$558, last 12 months +$289, every one of 9 quarters positive, 16 of 25 months positive, buys 1.25 (110) / **sells 2.40 (79)**, always-buy twin 0.69, random 0.98, max DD −$92, worst trade −$59, average risk $8.9 (9% of the account), longest losing run 9; n 3: 295 trades, PF 1.32, years 1.39 / 1.26, +$584, DD −$202. Robustness (median PF of the 30 entry × TP × n cells): entry TF M5 / M10 / M15 / M20 / M30 / H1 → 0.89 / 1.17 / 1.33 / 1.48 / 1.03 / 1.22 (43 trades) — a plateau M10-M20; killzone LDN+NY / NY / LDN / all day / **FAKE 02-05 + 19-22 → 1.33 / 1.72 / 0.91 / 1.03 / 0.85** (the NY window does the work); BOS + CHoCH 1.20, CHoCH only 1.05; order life 10 / 30 / 60 bars 1.36 / 1.33 / 1.33; hold 6 / 12 / 24 h 1.32 / 1.33 / 1.32; SL buffer 0 / 0.1 / 0.25 / 0.5 ATR 1.32 / 1.33 / 1.35 / 1.50. Caveats: gold only (the same cell on all 29 symbols PF 0.81), found among ~1,400 gold variants, year-1 picks → year 2 median 1.00. **Live as v2.23 (`FizySMC.mqh`) on the owner's order.**
    - **Round 103 / 103b, BTCUSD: why it is hard and what works** (`research/btc103.py`, `btc103b.py`, 13 Nov, last 2 years, data run 43201971006; owner: "cari macam mana nak trade btc. kenapa ia agak susah nak bina"). BTCUSD.m: contract 1 BTC, so 0.01 lot = $0.01 per $1 of BTC; median price 88,346, spread $20.19 = $0.20 per 0.01 lot. **Why it is hard, measured:** (1) **swap long −$0.85 / short −$0.56 per 0.01 lot per night = 35% / 23% of the price a year (gold 6-8%), 2-3% of a median D1 ATR every night**, and BTC trades 7 days (28% of the M5 bars are Saturday / Sunday), so every hold past midnight bleeds (D1 20/10 breakout: 22 trades, −$341 of which −$324 swap; rounds 87 / 98 held 8-48 h and scored 0.35-0.83); (2) **the spread is 14.7% of an M5 ATR and 8.0% of an M15 ATR (gold 5.0% / 2.7%, EURUSD 27.6% / 15.0%)**, 3.8% of H1, 0.7% of D1; (3) **no stable behaviour**: variance ratio D1 q20 0.54 in year 1 (reverting) vs 1.09 in year 2, M5 / H1 0.86-1.00; daily autocorrelation −0.11 / −0.04; time-of-day 146 cells, none with year-1 t ≥ 2, 48 of 146 positive in year 2; weekday returns flip between years; BTC 88k → 126k → 58k → 73k over the window. Families that are break-even and equal to their random twin (intraday, out 23:30): session range breakout PF 0.89-1.15 (rand 0.81-1.25), H1 Donchian 0.84-1.06, H1 RSI2 / BB mean reversion 0.92-1.01, Williams breakout 0.87-1.06; M5 spike FADE (gold's edge) 0.45-0.68. **Two survivors.** (A) **M5 big-candle FOLLOW** (|bar| ≥ 2.5 ATR(M5), gate 4, enter its way, SL 0.3 ATR beyond the bar, out after 12 bars): 496 trades, PF 1.21, years 1.16 / 1.26, +$214, DD −$80; bands on their own 2.0-2.5 / 2.5-3.0 / 3.0-3.5 / 3.5-5 → 1.00 / 1.14 / 1.59 / 1.35; gate 0-12 1.21-1.45, hold 12-48 1.20-1.23, SL 0-1 ATR 1.16-1.24 — **but the weekend carries it (weekend 1.79, weekdays 1.12 = fail) and so do sells in a falling year (sells 1.32, buys 1.10, buys year 2 0.92)**; not recommended. (B) **SMC BOS + limit at the OTE 0.705 in the killzone, weekdays (round 102 rules)**: centre **M20 n3 TP 3R: 201 trades (1.9 a week), PF 1.51, years 1.37 / 1.71, stress 1.45, +$197 per 0.01 lot, last 12 months +$117, buys 1.86 / sells 1.12, random twin 1.11, always-buy 0.91, max DD −$39, worst trade −$8, average risk $2.9, 13 of 25 months positive, longest losing run 10**; plateau weekdays killzone M10 n5 / M15 n3 / M15 n5 / M20 n3 × TP 2R / 3R / liquidity 1.18-1.51 (M10 n3 1.08-1.10); London 09-12 1.37-1.50, NY 14-17 1.18-1.36, all day 0.94-1.07, **FAKE 02-05 + 19-22 0.95-1.08**, Asia 0.79-1.06; entry OTE 1.51 vs OB top 1.00 (gold's entry), OB 50% 1.21, FVG top 1.34 (89 trades). **Built 24/7 as v2.25 (`FizyBTC.mqh`, round 103c: FOL + SMC weekdays, 0.01 lot each).**

(2) **RANGE-EXHAUSTION FADE**: once today's range from 01:30 reaches X × ATR14(D1) at a new high (low), sell (buy) at the next M1 open, SL / TP 0.3 × ATR(D1), out after 120 min: X 0.8 / 0.9 / 1.0 / 1.1 / 1.2 → 1.00-1.17 / 1.45-1.56 / 1.34-1.53 / 1.08-1.16 / 1.26-1.30; X 1.0: 219 trades (2.1 a week), years 1.29 / 1.69, +$633, last 12 months +$497, DD −$110, worst day −$57, risk $22.6; X 0.9 SL 0.2 TP 0.3: 274 trades, PF 1.47, years 1.23 / 1.62, DD −$96, worst day −$39, 20 of 25 months; **buys 2.01 (fading a down day) vs sells 1.12**, always-buy twin 1.36, fires mostly 15-18 server (PF 1.67) vs 18-24 (1.01). It is an always-in **trailing stop-and-reverse** with a tight gap; at 0.5 lot (50 × ours) a $2 move shows as $100. Simulated with the real spread, stops frozen at the bar start, at most one flip per bar (UNDER-counts flips and spread when the gap is smaller than the bar, so the tight gaps are an optimistic upper bound). **Every variant loses or is flat**: gap $0.3 / 0.5 / 1 / 2 / 3 / 5 all day → PF 0.79 / 0.83 / 0.89 / 0.95 / 0.99 / 1.01, $ per 0.01 lot −$65,167 / −$52,103 / −$30,098 / −$9,728 / −$1,653 / +$1,350 in 2 years at 896 / 797 / 579 / 323 / 199 / 96 flips a day, win 32-38%, spread paid $103,097 / … / $12,020; k × ATR(M1) 0.5 / 1 / 2 / 3 → 0.87 / 0.94 / 0.96 / 0.96; NY 15:30-18:00 only 0.84-1.01; cost gate ≥ 8 only 0.87-1.02 (best $5: +$1,880 in 2 years, year 1 0.96, last 3 months −$245, DD −$1,853). The $0.3-1 gaps lose $30-65 k a year per 0.01 lot: at 0.5 lot that is $1.5-3.3 M, i.e. any account is gone on day 1. Same answer as round 46 (every minute) and round 110's UT Bot (PF 0.81). A BE exit earns about −spread to +$1 and then reverses into a fresh trade with a $5 stop, so a choppy day flips 2-3 times as often; the $25 target never fires on stop exits (a BE stop is never $25 above entry), so its rows equal the no-target rows.     - **Round 115 / 115b, the LAST H1 CANDLE of the day as a base zone on gold** (`research/base115.py`, `base115b.py`, 15 Nov, data run 43620182422; owner: "teknik candle terakhir close sebagai base dan cara entry keesokan nya atau bila2 apabila price kembali ke area tu. base tu di tanda pada tf h1, antara wick atas bawah"). Zone = high / low of an H1 bar of the day; from 01:30 server of the next trading day read the side (wait outside the zone), then on the first RETURN: BOUNCE (above → BUY LIMIT at the zone high, below → SELL LIMIT at the zone low, SL beyond the other side + spread + 0.1 × height) or BREAK (stop at the far side, SL beyond the near side); TP 1R / 2R / 3R / none, out 23:30 server of the fill day; zone valid 1 / 3 / 5 / 10 days, one trade per zone; controls fake zones shifted ±0.5-2 heights and a RANDOM H1 bar of the day. About 4 trades a week, risk $7-11. **23:00 base BOUNCE with TP 1-3R loses or is flat (0.92-1.12); with no TP (hold to 23:30) PF 1.14-1.28: valid 10 d 442 trades, PF 1.28, years 1.32 / 1.26, stress 1.26, fake zones 1.12, buys 1.20 / sells 1.39, +$931, last 12 months +$569, last 3 months +$164, previous 3 +$300, DD −$383, worst day −$69 — but the 23:00 bar holds the rollover minutes: 23:00-23:44 only → 1.15, years 1.13 / 1.17, +$477, DD −$441.** Neighbouring base bars, BOUNCE TP none valid 10 d: 18 / 19 / 20 / 21 / 22 / 23 h → 0.94 / 1.13 (year 1 0.96) / 1.16 / 1.24 / 1.07 (year 1 0.95) / 1.28; random H1 bar 0.87 / 1.07 / 0.87 — evening bars beat a random bar by about +0.2 but bumpily (22:00 is a hole). 22:00 base BOUNCE 0.75-1.07. **BREAK is not about the base**: 22:00 BREAK TP 2R 1.34-1.38 but year 1 0.97-1.00; a RANDOM H1 bar's zone breaks just as well (1.08-1.22), i.e. round 51's stretch-continues effect. Nothing built; offered: the 23:00-23:44 bounce held to 23:30 is a thin pass (PF 1.15) with bumpy neighbours. **Round 115c, the direction from the D1 COLOUR** (`research/base115c.py`, run 43672183434; owner: "kalau h1 base tu di bawah iaitu d1 bearish, dia akan jadi buy"): day closed red → BUY LIMIT at the base high once price is above it; green → SELL LIMIT at the base low once below (D1-BOUNCE); D1-ANY = first touch from either side; controls OPPOSITE colour and RANDOM colour (3 seeds). **23:00 base, TP none (out 23:30), valid 5 d: 422 trades (4.1 a week), win 22%, PF 1.73, years 1.27 / 1.95, stress 1.68, buys 1.78 / sells 1.69, +$2,089, last 12 months +$1,828, last 3 months +$517, previous 3 −$23, DD −$266, worst day −$69**; valid 1 / 3 / 10 d 1.67 / 1.68 / 1.70; 23:00-23:44 base (no rollover minutes) 1.57-1.62, years 1.22-1.27 / 1.71-1.80, +$1,427-1,683; 21:00 base 1.48-1.57 (years 1.10-1.29 / 1.62-1.77); **22:00 base 1.32-1.40 but year 1 0.91-1.00 (fails)**; TP 2R 1.16-1.39 (23:00 1.27-1.33, DD −$165 to −$218); D1-ANY ≈ or below D1-BOUNCE; base in the day's 30% end 1.17-1.66 on ~250 trades. **Controls, TP none: OPPOSITE colour 0.86-1.01, RANDOM colour 1.09-1.21** → the colour adds +0.4-0.6 PF at 21:00 / 23:00. Caveats: the profit is mostly year 2 (12 months +$1,828 of +$2,089), the 22:00 hole, and it may be plain next-day reversion after a red / green day rather than the base (a random-level control not yet run). Passes the rule; offered, not built. **Round 115d, base or colour?** (`research/base115d.py`, run 43679574092; owner: "cuba dulu"): same colour direction, D1-BOUNCE, TP none, valid 5 d, the level replaced. 23:00 base: REAL 1.73 (+$2,089, DD −$266); zone shifted −2 / −1 / −0.5 / +0.5 / +1 / +2 heights → 1.09 / 1.08 / 1.45 / 1.27 / 1.29 / 1.02; a RANDOM H1 bar 02-20 of the day (3 seeds) 1.48 / 1.60 / 1.20; **NO LEVEL, market order at the next 01:30 open against the D1 colour, SL = base height × 1.1 + spread, out 23:30: 457 trades, PF 1.59, years 1.50 / 1.64, stress 1.59, +$2,022, 12 months +$1,449, last 3 months +$944, previous 3 +$38, DD −$245, worst day −$73**; market at a random minute 01:30-20:00 1.26-1.35. 23:00-23:44: REAL 1.62, shifts 1.02-1.48, MKT 01:30 1.57. 21:00: REAL 1.55, shifts 1.05-1.47, random H1 1.20-1.59, MKT 01:30 1.29. **So the edge is mostly the D1 colour (next-day reversal: buy the day after a red day, sell after a green one, entered early) — the base adds about +0.1-0.15 PF over no level, and levels within ±0.5 height of it do nearly as well.** Offered: the base version or the simpler 01:30 market version; nothing built. **Round 115e, validating an older base** (`research/base115e.py`, run 43680829734; owner chose the base version and asked how to validate yesterday's / older bases): 23:00 base, TP none. **By age at the fill: 385 of 422 fills (91%) are on the next trading day (PF 1.67, years 1.30 / 1.86); age 2 / 3 / 4-5 days only 21 / 5 / 11 trades in 2 years (PF 1.52 / 30 / 2.87, all year 2)** — older bases are rare and too few to judge. Random H1 bar of the same day with the same colour rule: ≥ 0.5 ATR 1.11 / 1.29 / 1.51 against the real base ≥ 0.5 ATR 1.94. Other splits carry nothing usable: session of the fill (Asia 357 trades PF 1.83, London 22 trades 3.24, NY 15 trades 0.32), hours to the fill, excursion before the return (303 of 394 return within 1 base height), D1 body size (1.72-1.96 flat), close location of the base bar (1.96 / 1.13 / 2.32, not monotone), the next day's open vs the zone (all 1.60-2.37). On a driftless path any exit rule has an expected P&L of minus the costs, so a breakeven ladder with a $1 distance cannot be judged on M1 bars — it needs tick data. Behaviour, last 2 years: it moves in the NY hours (median H1 range $5.0-6.7 at 0.01 lot from 21:00 to 01:00 MYT, $4.4-5.0 from 16:00-18:00, $1.7-2.5 from 04:00 to 13:00); D1 ATR $20.7; price 68 → 87 with a 2026 spike to 113; **persistence flips between the years** (H4 VR 1.01-1.05 in year 1, 0.81-0.91 in year 2; D1 VR20 0.57 vs 0.98); a stretch from the EMA 50 predicts nothing (−0.21 to +0.17 ATR, sign flips by year); after a big day the next day is flat in year 1 and reverses in year 2 (k 1.5: +0.02 vs −1.08 ATR, 6-9 days); **the day after an NR7 is QUIETER (0.89 ATR vs 1.01)**, so the NR7 breakout loses (PF 0.53). **0 of 47 pass**, median PF 0.99 vs random 0.92: D1 Donchian / EMA / 20-day momentum 0.68-1.33 on 8-53 trades; H4 Donchian 20 hold 12 bars 148 trades PF 1.19 but year 1 0.90; H4 Kumo 66 trades 1.24 (year 1 0.77); H1 Donchian 0.75-0.87; H1 Kumo 0.73-1.11; inside-day breakout 49 trades PF 1.65 (years 1.05 / 2.08, too few); SMC H1 0.65-1.49 (≤ 74 trades in the killzone), SMC H4 OB top 26-42 trades PF 1.45-2.31 with year 1 0.38-1.21. **Every positive cell is a 2026 (year 2) effect on fewer than 150 trades.** Nothing built.
